<div class="col-sm order-1">
      <h2 class="chapter_title">CHAPTER 149A. MORTUARY SCIENCE; DISPOSITION OF DEAD BODIES</h2>
      
      <div class="sr" id="stat.149A.001"><b>149A.001</b> MS 2006 [Renumbered <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/15.001">15.001</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.149A.01">
        <h1 class="shn">149A.01 PURPOSE AND SCOPE.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.01.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Purpose.</span></h2>
          <p>This chapter regulates the removal, preparation, transportation, arrangements for disposition, and final disposition of dead human bodies for purposes of public health and protection of the public.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.01.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Scope.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">In Minnesota no person shall, without being licensed by the commissioner of health:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) take charge of or remove from the place of death a dead human body;</p>
          <p class="in">(2) prepare a dead human body for final disposition, in any manner; or</p>
          <p class="in">(3) arrange, direct, or supervise a funeral, memorial service, or graveside service.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.01.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Exceptions to licensure.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) Except as otherwise provided in this chapter, nothing in this chapter shall in any way interfere with the duties of:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) an anatomical bequest program located within an accredited school of medicine or an accredited college of mortuary science;</p>
          <p class="in">(2) a person engaged in the performance of duties prescribed by law relating to the conditions under which unclaimed dead human bodies are held subject to anatomical study;</p>
          <p class="in">(3) authorized personnel from a licensed ambulance service in the performance of their duties;</p>
          <p class="in">(4) licensed medical personnel in the performance of their duties; or</p>
          <p class="in">(5) the coroner or medical examiner in the performance of the duties of their offices.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) This chapter does not apply to or interfere with the recognized customs or rites of any culture or recognized religion in the ceremonial washing, dressing, casketing, and public transportation of their dead, to the extent that all other provisions of this chapter are complied with.</p>
          <p class="in">(c) Noncompensated persons with the right to control the dead human body, under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/149A.80#stat.149A.80.2">149A.80, subdivision 2</a>, may remove a body from the place of death; transport the body; prepare the body for disposition, except embalming; or arrange for final disposition of the body, provided that all actions are in compliance with this chapter.</p>
          <p class="in">(d) Persons serving internships pursuant to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/149A.20#stat.149A.20.6">149A.20, subdivision 6</a>; students officially registered for a practicum or clinical through a program of mortuary science accredited by the American Board of Funeral Service Education; or transfer care specialists registered pursuant to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/149A.47">149A.47</a> are not required to be licensed, provided that the persons, students, or transfer care specialists are registered with the commissioner and act under the direct and exclusive supervision of a person holding a current license to practice mortuary science in Minnesota.</p>
          <p class="in">(e) Notwithstanding this subdivision, nothing in this section shall be construed to prohibit an institution or entity from establishing, implementing, or enforcing a policy that permits only persons licensed by the commissioner to remove or cause to be removed a dead body or body part from the institution or entity.</p>
          <p class="in">(f) An unlicensed person may arrange for and direct or supervise a memorial service if that person or that person's employer does not have charge of the dead human body. An unlicensed person may not take charge of the dead human body, unless that person has the right to control the dead human body under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/149A.80#stat.149A.80.2">149A.80, subdivision 2</a>, or is that person's noncompensated designee.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.01.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Nonlimiting.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) Nothing in this chapter shall be construed to limit the powers granted to the commissioner of health, commissioner of commerce, state attorney general, or a county attorney in any other statute, law, or rule, except as described in paragraph (b).</p>
          <p class="in">(b) A county, within its jurisdiction as a coroner or medical examiner, may establish transportation standards for transporting a dead human body from the death scene to the place where an autopsy is to be conducted, so long as the standards do not specifically require that the transporter be a licensed funeral director.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.149A.01.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/215/#laws.0.5.0">1997 c 215 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/0/171/#laws.0.5.0">2001 c 171 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/114/#laws.0.1.0">2007 c 114 s 1</a>,2; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2008/0/189/#laws.0.21.0">2008 c 189 s 21</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2010/0/262/#laws.0.1.0">2010 c 262 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/127/#laws.18.1.0">2024 c 127 art 18 s 1</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.149A.02">
        <h1 class="shn">149A.02 DEFINITIONS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.02.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Scope.</span></h2>
          <p>For purposes of this chapter, the terms defined in this section have the meanings given them.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.02.1a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 1a.<span class="headnote">Alkaline hydrolysis.</span></h2>
          <p>"Alkaline hydrolysis" means the reduction of a dead human body to essential elements through a water-based dissolution process using alkaline chemicals, heat, agitation, and pressure to accelerate natural decomposition; the processing of the hydrolyzed remains after removal from the alkaline hydrolysis vessel; placement of the processed remains in a hydrolyzed remains container; and release of the hydrolyzed remains to an appropriate party. Alkaline hydrolysis is a form of final disposition.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.02.1b">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 1b.<span class="headnote">Alkaline hydrolysis container.</span></h2>
          <p>"Alkaline hydrolysis container" means a hydrolyzable or biodegradable closed container or pouch resistant to leakage of bodily fluids that encases the body and into which a dead human body is placed prior to insertion into an alkaline hydrolysis vessel. Alkaline hydrolysis containers may be hydrolyzable or biodegradable alternative containers or caskets.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.02.1c">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 1c.<span class="headnote">Alkaline hydrolysis facility.</span></h2>
          <p>"Alkaline hydrolysis facility" means a building or structure containing one or more alkaline hydrolysis vessels for the alkaline hydrolysis of dead human bodies.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.02.1d">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 1d.<span class="headnote">Alkaline hydrolysis vessel.</span></h2>
          <p>"Alkaline hydrolysis vessel" means the container in which the alkaline hydrolysis of a dead human body is performed.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.02.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Alternative container.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">"Alternative container" means a nonmetal receptacle or enclosure, without ornamentation or a fixed interior lining, which is designed for the encasement of dead human bodies and is made of hydrolyzable or biodegradable materials, corrugated cardboard, fiberboard, pressed-wood, or other like materials.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.02.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Arrangements for disposition.</span></h2>
          <p>"Arrangements for disposition" means any action normally taken by a funeral provider in anticipation of or preparation for the entombment, burial in a cemetery, alkaline hydrolysis, cremation, or, effective July 1, 2025, natural organic reduction of a dead human body.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.02.3a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3a.<span class="headnote">Burial site goods.</span></h2>
          <p>"Burial site goods" means any goods sold or offered for sale or rental directly to the public for use in connection with the final disposition of a dead human body.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.02.3b">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3b.<span class="headnote">Burial site services.</span></h2>
          <p>"Burial site services" means any services sold or offered for sale directly to the public for use in connection with the final disposition of a dead human body but does not include services provided under a transportation protection agreement.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.02.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Cash advance item.</span></h2>
          <p>"Cash advance item" means any item of service or merchandise described to a purchaser as a "cash advance," "accommodation," "cash disbursement," or similar term. A cash advance item is also any item obtained from a third party and paid for by the funeral provider on the purchaser's behalf. Cash advance items include, but are not limited to, cemetery, alkaline hydrolysis, or crematory services, pallbearers, public transportation, clergy honoraria, flowers, musicians or singers, obituary notices, gratuities, and death records.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.02.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Casket.</span></h2>
          <p>"Casket" means a rigid container which is designed for the encasement of a dead human body and is usually constructed of hydrolyzable or biodegradable materials, wood, metal, fiberglass, plastic, or like material, and ornamented and lined with fabric.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.02.5a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5a.<span class="headnote">Clinical student.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">"Clinical student" means a person officially registered for a clinical through a program of mortuary science accredited by the American Board of Funeral Service Education.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.02.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Commissioner.</span></h2>
          <p>"Commissioner" means the Minnesota commissioner of health.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.02.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Cremated remains.</span></h2>
          <p>"Cremated remains" means the postcremation remains of a dead human body.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.02.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Cremated remains container.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">"Cremated remains container" means a receptacle in which postcremation remains are placed. For purposes of this chapter, "cremated remains container" is interchangeable with "urn" or similar keepsake storage jewelry.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.02.9">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9.<span class="headnote">Cremation.</span></h2>
          <p>"Cremation" means the reduction of a dead human body to essential elements through direct exposure to intense heat and flame and the repositioning or movement of the body during the process to facilitate reduction, the processing of the remains after removal from the cremation chamber, placement of the processed remains in a cremated remains container, and release of the cremated remains to an appropriate party.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.02.10">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 10.<span class="headnote">Cremation chamber.</span></h2>
          <p>"Cremation chamber" means the enclosed space within which the cremation of a dead human body is performed.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.02.11">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 11.<span class="headnote">Cremation container.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">"Cremation container" means a combustible, closed container that encases the body and can be made of materials like fiberboard or corrugated cardboard and into which a dead human body is placed prior to insertion into a cremation chamber for cremation. Cremation containers may be combustible "alternative containers" or combustible "caskets."</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.02.12">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 12.<span class="headnote">Crematory.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">"Crematory" means a building or structure containing one or more cremation chambers or retorts for the cremation of dead human bodies.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.02.12a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 12a.<span class="headnote">Crypt.</span></h2>
          <p>"Crypt" means a space in a mausoleum of sufficient size, used or intended to be used to entomb human remains, cremated remains, or hydrolyzed remains.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.02.12b">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 12b.<span class="headnote">Direct alkaline hydrolysis.</span></h2>
          <p>"Direct alkaline hydrolysis" means a final disposition of a dead human body by alkaline hydrolysis, without formal viewing, visitation, or ceremony with the body present.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.02.13">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 13.<span class="headnote">Direct cremation.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">"Direct cremation" means a final disposition of a dead human body by cremation, without formal viewing, visitation, or ceremony with the body present.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.02.13a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 13a.<span class="headnote">Direct supervision.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">"Direct supervision" means overseeing the performance of an individual. For the purpose of a clinical, practicum, or internship, direct supervision means that the supervisor is available to observe and correct, as needed, the performance of the trainee. For the purpose of a transfer care specialist, direct supervision means that the supervisor is available by being physically present or by telephone to advise and correct, as needed, the performance of the transfer care specialist. The supervising mortician is accountable for the actions of the clinical student, practicum student, or intern throughout the course of the training. The supervising mortician is accountable for any violations of law or rule, in the performance of their duties, by the clinical student, practicum student, intern, or transfer care specialist.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.02.14">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 14.<span class="headnote">Disciplinary action.</span></h2>
          <p>"Disciplinary action" means any action taken by the regulatory agency against any person subject to regulation under this chapter for the violation of or the threatened violation of any law, rule, order, stipulation agreement, settlement, compliance agreement, license, or permit adopted, issued, or enforced by the regulatory agency.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.02.15">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 15.<span class="headnote">Embalming.</span></h2>
          <p>"Embalming" means the process of disinfecting and preserving a dead human body by chemically treating the body to reduce the presence and growth of organisms, to retard organic decomposition, and to restore an acceptable physical appearance.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.02.16">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 16.<span class="headnote">Final disposition.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">"Final disposition" means the acts leading to and the entombment, burial in a cemetery, alkaline hydrolysis, cremation, or, effective July 1, 2025, natural organic reduction of a dead human body.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.02.17">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 17.<span class="headnote">Funeral association.</span></h2>
          <p>"Funeral association" means a cooperative association that sells or offers to sell funeral goods or services to its members.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.02.18">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 18.<span class="headnote">Funeral ceremony.</span></h2>
          <p>"Funeral ceremony" means a service or rite commemorating the deceased with the body present.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.02.19">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 19.<span class="headnote">Funeral director.</span></h2>
          <p>"Funeral director" means any person who, for compensation, arranges, directs, or supervises funerals, memorial services, or graveside services, or engages in the business or practice of preparing dead human bodies for final disposition by means other than embalming.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.02.20">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 20.<span class="headnote">Funeral establishment.</span></h2>
          <p>"Funeral establishment" means any place or premise devoted to or used in the holding, care, or preparation of a dead human body for final disposition or any place used as the office or place of business of any person that provides funeral goods or services to the public.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.02.21">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 21.<span class="headnote">Funeral goods.</span></h2>
          <p>"Funeral goods" means the goods which are sold or offered for sale directly to the public for use in connection with funeral services.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.02.22">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 22.<span class="headnote">Funeral provider.</span></h2>
          <p>"Funeral provider" means any person that sells or offers to sell funeral goods, funeral services, burial site goods, or burial site services to the public. "Funeral provider" does not include monument builders who sell and install markers and headstones, with or without foundations, at retail to the public, but do not sell any other funeral good, funeral service, burial good, or burial site service.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.02.23">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 23.<span class="headnote">Funeral services.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) "Funeral services" means any services which may be used to: (1) care for and prepare dead human bodies for burial, alkaline hydrolysis, cremation, or other final disposition; and (2) arrange, supervise, or conduct the funeral ceremony or the final disposition of dead human bodies.</p>
          <p>(b) Funeral service does not include a transportation protection agreement.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.02.24">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 24.<span class="headnote">Graveside service.</span></h2>
          <p>"Graveside service" means a service or rite, conducted at the place of interment, commemorating the deceased with the body present.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.02.24a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 24a.<span class="headnote">Hydrolyzed remains.</span></h2>
          <p>"Hydrolyzed remains" means the remains of a dead human body following the alkaline hydrolysis process. Hydrolyzed remains does not include pacemakers, prostheses, or similar foreign materials.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.02.24b">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 24b.<span class="headnote">Hydrolyzed remains container.</span></h2>
          <p>"Hydrolyzed remains container" means a receptacle in which hydrolyzed remains are placed. For purposes of this chapter, a hydrolyzed remains container is interchangeable with "urn" or similar keepsake storage jewelry.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.02.25">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 25.<span class="headnote">Immediate burial.</span></h2>
          <p>"Immediate burial" means a disposition of a dead human body by burial, without formal viewing, visitation, or ceremony with the body present, except for a graveside service.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.02.26">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 26.<span class="headnote">Intern.</span></h2>
          <p>"Intern" means an individual that has met the educational and testing requirements for a license to practice mortuary science in Minnesota, has registered with the commissioner of health, and is engaged in the practice of mortuary science under the direction and supervision of a currently licensed Minnesota mortuary science practitioner.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.02.26a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 26a.<span class="headnote">Inurnment.</span></h2>
          <p>"Inurnment" means placing hydrolyzed or cremated remains in a hydrolyzed or cremated remains container suitable for placement, burial, or shipment. Effective July 1, 2025, inurnment also includes placing naturally reduced remains in a naturally reduced remains container suitable for placement, burial, or shipment.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.02.27">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 27.<span class="headnote">Licensee.</span></h2>
          <p>"Licensee" means any person or entity that has been issued a license to practice mortuary science, to operate a funeral establishment, to operate an alkaline hydrolysis facility, to operate a crematory, or, effective July 1, 2025, to operate a natural organic reduction facility by the Minnesota commissioner of health.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.02.28">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 28.<span class="headnote">Memorial service.</span></h2>
          <p>"Memorial service" means a ceremony or rite commemorating the deceased without the body present.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.02.29">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 29.<span class="headnote">Mortician.</span></h2>
          <p>"Mortician" means any person who, for compensation, practices the art of embalming and arranges, directs, or supervises funerals, memorial services, or graveside services, or engages in the business or practice of preparing dead human bodies for final disposition.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.02.30">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 30.<span class="headnote">Mortuary science.</span></h2>
          <p>"Mortuary science" means the study and practice of preparing dead human bodies for final disposition and providing funeral services. Mortuary science includes any conduct or action associated with the removal, preparation, transportation, arrangements for disposition, or final disposition of dead human bodies, including the actions and duties of a mortician or a funeral director.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.02.30a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 30a.<span class="headnote">Niche.</span></h2>
          <p>"Niche" means a space in a columbarium used or intended to be used for the placement of hydrolyzed or cremated remains.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.02.30b">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 30b.<span class="headnote">Natural organic reduction or naturally reduce.</span></h2>
          <p>"Natural organic reduction" or "naturally reduce" means the contained, accelerated conversion of a dead human body to soil. This subdivision is effective July 1, 2025.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.02.30c">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 30c.<span class="headnote">Natural organic reduction facility.</span></h2>
          <p>"Natural organic reduction facility" means a structure, room, or other space in a building or real property where natural organic reduction of a dead human body occurs. This subdivision is effective July 1, 2025.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.02.30d">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 30d.<span class="headnote">Natural organic reduction vessel.</span></h2>
          <p>"Natural organic reduction vessel" means the enclosed container in which natural organic reduction takes place. This subdivision is effective July 1, 2025.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.02.30e">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 30e.<span class="headnote">Naturally reduced remains.</span></h2>
          <p>"Naturally reduced remains" means the soil remains following the natural organic reduction of a dead human body and the accompanying plant material. This subdivision is effective July 1, 2025.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.02.30f">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 30f.<span class="headnote">Naturally reduced remains container.</span></h2>
          <p>"Naturally reduced remains container" means a receptacle in which naturally reduced remains are placed. This subdivision is effective July 1, 2025.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.02.31">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 31.<span class="headnote">Outer burial container.</span></h2>
          <p>"Outer burial container" means any container which is designed for placement in the grave around a casket or alternative container including, but not limited to, containers commonly known as burial vaults, grave boxes, and grave liners.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.02.32">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 32.<span class="headnote">Person.</span></h2>
          <p>"Person" means an individual or a firm, corporation, limited liability company, partnership, association, or other legal entity.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.02.32a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 32a.<span class="headnote">Placement.</span></h2>
          <p>"Placement" means the placing of a container holding hydrolyzed or cremated remains in a crypt, vault, or niche.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.02.33">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 33.<span class="headnote">Practicum student.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">"Practicum student" means a person officially registered for a practicum through a program of mortuary science accredited by the American Board of Funeral Service Education.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.02.33a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 33a.<span class="headnote">Preneed consumer.</span></h2>
          <p>"Preneed consumer" means an individual who arranges for funeral goods, funeral services, burial site goods, or burial site services prior to the death of that individual or another individual, and who funds those goods or services through prepayment to a funeral provider or through purchase of an insurance policy.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.02.34">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 34.<span class="headnote">Preparation of the body.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">"Preparation of the body" means placement of the body into an appropriate cremation or alkaline hydrolysis container, embalming of the body or such items of care as washing, disinfecting, shaving, positioning of features, restorative procedures, application of cosmetics, dressing, and casketing.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.02.35">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 35.<span class="headnote">Processing.</span></h2>
          <p>"Processing" means the removal of foreign objects, drying or cooling, and the reduction of the hydrolyzed remains, cremated remains, or, effective July 1, 2025, naturally reduced remains by mechanical means including, but not limited to, grinding, crushing, or pulverizing, to a granulated appearance appropriate for final disposition or the final reduction to naturally reduced remains.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.02.36">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 36.<span class="headnote">Professional services.</span></h2>
          <p>"Professional services" means the basic services of the funeral director or mortician and staff that are furnished by the funeral provider in arranging final disposition. The services include, but are not limited to, conducting the arrangement conference; planning visitations and the funeral, memorial service, or graveside service; arranging for final disposition by securing, preparing, and filing necessary permits and documents; and placing obituary notices.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.02.37">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 37.<span class="headnote">Public transportation.</span></h2>
          <p>"Public transportation" means all manner of transportation via common carrier available to the general public including airlines, buses, railroads, and ships. For purposes of this chapter, a livery service providing transportation to private funeral establishments, alkaline hydrolysis facilities, or crematories is not public transportation.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.02.37a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 37a.<span class="headnote">Regulatory agency.</span></h2>
          <p>"Regulatory agency" means:</p>
          <p>(1) the commissioner of health for provisions related to a funeral provider who is required to be licensed, registered, or issued a permit under this chapter; and</p>
          <p>(2) the commissioner of commerce for provisions related to insurance policies purchased by a preneed consumer to arrange for funeral goods, funeral services, burial site goods, or burial services.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.02.37b">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 37b.<span class="headnote">Refrigeration.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">"Refrigeration" means to preserve by keeping cool at a temperature of 40 degrees Fahrenheit or less using mechanical or natural means.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.02.37c">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 37c.<span class="headnote">Scattering.</span></h2>
          <p>"Scattering" means the authorized dispersal of hydrolyzed remains, cremated remains, or, effective July 1, 2025, naturally reduced remains in a defined area of a dedicated cemetery or in areas where no local prohibition exists provided that the hydrolyzed, cremated, or naturally reduced remains are not distinguishable to the public, are not in a container, and that the person who has control over disposition of the hydrolyzed, cremated, or naturally reduced remains has obtained written permission of the property owner or governing agency to scatter on the property.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.02.37d">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 37d.<span class="headnote">Transfer care specialist.</span></h2>
          <p>"Transfer care specialist" means an individual who is registered with the commissioner in accordance with section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/149A.47">149A.47</a> and is authorized to perform the removal of a dead human body from the place of death under the direct supervision of a licensed mortician.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.02.38">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 38.<span class="headnote">Transportation costs.</span></h2>
          <p>"Transportation costs" means all costs associated with the use of the vehicle used for the initial transfer of the deceased, the funeral coach, funeral sedans, the flower car, any service or utility vehicles, and public transportation.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.02.38a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 38a.<span class="headnote">Transportation protection agreement.</span></h2>
          <p>"Transportation protection agreement" means an agreement that is primarily for the purpose of transportation and subsequent transportation of the remains of a dead human body.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.02.39">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 39.<span class="headnote">Universal precautions.</span></h2>
          <p>"Universal precautions" means the universal blood and body fluid precautions recommended by the United States Public Health Service, Centers for Disease Control, to prevent transmission of blood-borne and body fluid-borne infectious diseases.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.02.40">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 40.<span class="headnote">Use of facilities.</span></h2>
          <p>"Use of facilities" means the provision of a chapel or room for visitation, provision of a chapel or room for the funeral or memorial service, provision of facilities for parking, or provision of office space for administrative or planning purposes.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.02.41">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 41.<span class="headnote">Vault.</span></h2>
          <p>"Vault" means a space in a mausoleum of sufficient size, used or intended to be used to entomb human remains, cremated remains, or hydrolyzed remains. Vault may also mean a sealed and lined casket enclosure.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.149A.02.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/215/#laws.0.6.0">1997 c 215 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2000/0/438/#laws.0.1.0">2000 c 438 s 1</a>-4; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/0/171/#laws.0.6.0">2001 c 171 s 6</a>,7; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/1/9/#laws.15.32.0">1Sp2001 c 9 art 15 s 32</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/32/#laws.0.1.0">2003 c 32 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/114/#laws.0.3.0">2007 c 114 s 3</a>-13; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/108/#laws.12.50.0">2013 c 108 art 12 s 50</a>-72; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/127/#laws.18.2.0">2024 c 127 art 18 s 2</a>,3; art 58 s 21-31; art 59 s 48-50</p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.149A.025"><b>149A.025</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/108/#laws.12.109.0">2013 c 108 art 12 s 109</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.149A.03">
        <h1 class="shn">149A.03 DUTIES OF COMMISSIONER.</h1>
        <p class="in">The commissioner shall:</p>
        <p class="in">(1) enforce all laws and adopt and enforce rules relating to the:</p>
        <p class="in">(i) removal, preparation, transportation, arrangements for disposition, and final disposition of dead human bodies;</p>
        <p class="in">(ii) licensure, registration, and professional conduct of funeral directors, morticians, interns, practicum students, clinical students, and transfer care specialists;</p>
        <p class="in">(iii) licensing and operation of a funeral establishment;</p>
        <p>(iv) licensing and operation of an alkaline hydrolysis facility;</p>
        <p class="in">(v) licensing and operation of a crematory; and</p>
        <p>(vi) effective July 1, 2025, licensing and operation of a natural organic reduction facility;</p>
        <p class="in">(2) provide copies of the requirements for licensure, registration, and permits to all applicants;</p>
        <p class="in">(3) administer examinations and issue licenses, registrations, and permits to qualified persons and other legal entities;</p>
        <p class="in">(4) maintain a record of the name and location of all current licensees, interns, and transfer care specialists;</p>
        <p class="in">(5) perform periodic compliance reviews and premise inspections of licensees;</p>
        <p class="in">(6) accept and investigate complaints relating to conduct governed by this chapter;</p>
        <p class="in">(7) maintain a record of all current preneed arrangement trust accounts;</p>
        <p class="in">(8) maintain a schedule of application, examination, permit, registration, and licensure fees, initial and renewal, sufficient to cover all necessary operating expenses;</p>
        <p class="in">(9) educate the public about the existence and content of the laws and rules for mortuary science licensing and the removal, preparation, transportation, arrangements for disposition, and final disposition of dead human bodies to enable consumers to file complaints against licensees and others who may have violated those laws or rules;</p>
        <p class="in">(10) evaluate the laws, rules, and procedures regulating the practice of mortuary science in order to refine the standards for licensing and to improve the regulatory and enforcement methods used; and</p>
        <p class="in">(11) initiate proceedings to address and remedy deficiencies and inconsistencies in the laws, rules, or procedures governing the practice of mortuary science and the removal, preparation, transportation, arrangements for disposition, and final disposition of dead human bodies.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.149A.03.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/215/#laws.0.7.0">1997 c 215 s 7</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/114/#laws.0.14.0">2007 c 114 s 14</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/108/#laws.12.73.0">2013 c 108 art 12 s 73</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/127/#laws.18.4.0">2024 c 127 art 18 s 4</a>; art 58 s 32</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.149A.04">
        <h1 class="shn">149A.04 ENFORCEMENT.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.04.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">General authority.</span></h2>
          <p>The provisions of this chapter and all laws, now in force or later enacted, rules, orders, stipulation agreements, settlements, compliance agreements, licenses, and permits adopted or issued for the regulation of the removal, preparation, transportation, arrangements for disposition, or final disposition of dead human bodies or for the regulation of the practice of mortuary science may be enforced under this section.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.04.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Inspections.</span></h2>
          <p>The regulatory agency or a designee of the regulatory agency shall conduct initial licensure inspections and corrective order reinspections. As a condition of continued licensure, compliance reviews or premises inspections of licensees shall be conducted by the regulatory agency or a designee of the regulatory agency at least biennially. A compliance review or an inspection of the premises of any person subject to regulation under this chapter may also be conducted when the regulatory agency reasonably suspects that there may be a violation or a threat of a violation of any law now in force or later enacted, rule, order, stipulation agreement, settlement, compliance agreement, license, or permit issued by the regulatory agency. When possible, all inspections and compliance reviews shall be conducted without prior notice to the subject of the inspection or review and shall be performed in the presence of the subject of the inspection or review or an authorized representative of the subject of the inspection or review.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.04.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Access to information and property.</span></h2>
          <p>The regulatory agency or a designee of the regulatory agency, upon presentation of credentials, may:</p>
          <p>(1) examine and copy any relevant books, papers, records, memoranda, or data of any person subject to regulation under this chapter; and</p>
          <p>(2) enter upon any property, public or private, for the purpose of taking any action authorized under this chapter or rules, orders, stipulation agreements, settlements, compliance agreements, licenses, or permits adopted or issued by the regulatory agency, including obtaining information from a person that has a duty to provide information under this chapter or conducting inspections or investigations.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.04.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Subpoena power.</span></h2>
          <p>The regulatory agency may, as part of an investigation, issue subpoenas to require the attendance and testimony of witnesses and production of books, records, correspondence, and other information relevant to any matter involved in the investigation. The regulatory agency or the regulatory agency's designee may administer oaths to witnesses or take their affirmation. The subpoenas may be served upon any person named therein anywhere in the state by any person authorized to serve subpoenas or other processes in civil actions of the district courts. If a person to whom a subpoena is issued does not comply with the subpoena, the regulatory agency may apply to the district court in any district and the court may order compliance with the subpoena. Failure to obey the order of the court may be punished as contempt of court.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.04.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Data on investigations and disciplinary actions.</span></h2>
          <p>Data relating to any disciplinary measures or actions anticipated or taken by the regulatory agency are classified as follows:</p>
          <p>(1) data on persons other than individuals are civil investigative data under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/13.39">13.39</a>;</p>
          <p>(2) data on individuals are licensing data under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/13.41">13.41</a>; and</p>
          <p>(3) data on individuals who submit complaints to the regulatory agency regarding activities or practices regulated under this chapter are confidential data on individuals while an investigation is active and private data on individuals when an investigation becomes inactive.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.04.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Cooperation with other authorities.</span></h2>
          <p>The regulatory agency shall encourage and promote cooperation between and among other state and federal authorities where there is concurrent or overlapping enforcement or licensing jurisdiction.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.149A.04.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/215/#laws.0.8.0">1997 c 215 s 8</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/0/171/#laws.0.13.0">2001 c 171 s 13</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.149A.05">
        <h1 class="shn">149A.05 CORRECTION ORDERS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.05.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Authorization.</span></h2>
          <p>The regulatory agency may issue correction orders that require a person subject to regulation under this chapter to correct violations of this chapter or rules, orders, stipulation agreements, settlements, compliance agreements, licenses, and permits adopted or issued by the regulatory agency.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.05.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Contents of order.</span></h2>
          <p>The correction order must include:</p>
          <p>(1) a concise statement of the deficiencies alleged to constitute the violation;</p>
          <p>(2) a reference to the section of law, rule, order, stipulation agreement, settlement, compliance agreement, license, or permit that has been violated;</p>
          <p>(3) a statement of the time by and the manner in which the violation must be corrected; and</p>
          <p>(4) a statement of the right to request a hearing under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/14.57">14.57</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/14.62">14.62</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.05.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Request for hearing; hearing; and final order.</span></h2>
          <p>A request for hearing must be in writing, delivered to the regulatory agency by certified mail within 20 calendar days after the receipt of the correction order, and specifically state the reasons for seeking review of the order. The regulatory agency must initiate a hearing within 30 calendar days from the date of receipt of the written request for hearing. The hearing shall be conducted pursuant to the contested case procedures in sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/14.57">14.57</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/14.62">14.62</a>. No earlier than ten calendar days after and within 30 calendar days of receipt of the presiding administrative law judge's report, the regulatory agency shall issue a final order modifying, vacating, or making permanent the correction order as the facts require. If, within 20 calendar days of receipt of the correction order, the person that is the subject of the order fails to request a hearing in writing, the correction order becomes the final order of the regulatory agency.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.05.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Review of final order.</span></h2>
          <p>A judicial review of the final order issued by the regulatory agency subsequent to a contested case hearing may be requested in the manner prescribed in sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/14.63">14.63</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/14.69">14.69</a>. Failure to request a contested case hearing pursuant to subdivision 3 shall constitute a waiver of the right to further agency or judicial review of the final order.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.05.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Reinspections and effect of noncompliance.</span></h2>
          <p>If upon reinspection, or in the determination of the regulatory agency, it is found that any deficiency specified in a correction order has not been corrected by the person that is subject to the correction order, that person or entity is in noncompliance. The regulatory agency shall issue a notice of noncompliance and may impose any additional remedy available under this chapter.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.149A.05.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/215/#laws.0.9.0">1997 c 215 s 9</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/0/171/#laws.0.13.0">2001 c 171 s 13</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.149A.06">
        <h1 class="shn">149A.06 ADMINISTRATIVE PENALTY ORDERS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.06.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Authorization.</span></h2>
          <p>The regulatory agency may issue an order requiring violations to be corrected and administratively assessing monetary penalties for violations of this chapter or rules, orders, stipulation agreements, settlements, compliance agreements, licenses, and permits adopted, enforced, or issued by the regulatory agency.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.06.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Contents of order.</span></h2>
          <p>An order assessing an administrative penalty under this section must include:</p>
          <p>(1) a concise statement of the facts alleged to constitute a violation;</p>
          <p>(2) a reference to the section of law, rule, order, stipulation agreement, settlement, compliance agreement, license, or permit that has been violated;</p>
          <p>(3) a statement of the amount of the administrative penalty to be imposed and the factors upon which the penalty is based; and</p>
          <p>(4) a statement of the right to request a hearing pursuant to sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/14.57">14.57</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/14.62">14.62</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.06.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Concurrent corrective order.</span></h2>
          <p>The regulatory agency may issue an order assessing an administrative penalty and requiring the violations cited in the order to be corrected within 30 calendar days from the date the order is received. The subject of the order shall provide to the regulatory agency before the 31st day after the order was received, information demonstrating that the violation has been corrected or that a corrective plan, acceptable to the regulatory agency, has been developed. The regulatory agency shall determine whether the violation has been corrected and notify the subject of the order of the regulatory agency's determination.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.06.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Penalty.</span></h2>
          <p>If the regulatory agency determines that the violation has been corrected or an acceptable corrective plan has been developed, the penalty may be forgiven, except, where there are repeated or serious violations, the regulatory agency may issue an order with a penalty that will not be forgiven after corrective action is taken. Unless there is a request for review of the order under subdivision 6 before the penalty is due, the penalty is due and payable:</p>
          <p>(1) on the 31st calendar day after the order was received, if the subject of the order fails to provide information to the regulatory agency showing that the violation has been corrected or that appropriate steps have been taken toward correcting the violation;</p>
          <p>(2) on the 20th day after the subject of the order receives the regulatory agency's determination that the information provided is not sufficient to show that the violation has been corrected or that appropriate steps have been taken toward correcting the violation; or</p>
          <p>(3) on the 31st day after the order was received where the penalty is for repeated or serious violations and according to the order issued, the penalty will not be forgiven after corrective action is taken.</p>
          <p>All penalties due under this section are payable to the commissioner of management and budget, state of Minnesota, and shall be credited to the state government special revenue fund in the state treasury.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.06.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Amount of penalty; considerations.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The maximum amount of administrative penalty orders is $10,000 for each specific violation identified in an inspection, investigation, or compliance review.</p>
          <p>(b) In determining the amount of the administrative penalty, the regulatory agency shall consider the following:</p>
          <p>(1) the willfulness of the violation;</p>
          <p>(2) the gravity of the violation;</p>
          <p>(3) the history of past violations;</p>
          <p>(4) the number of violations;</p>
          <p>(5) the economic benefit gained by the person allowing or committing the violation; and</p>
          <p>(6) other factors as justice may require, if the regulatory agency specifically identifies the additional factors in the regulatory agency's order.</p>
          <p>(c) In determining the amount of a penalty for a violation subsequent to an initial violation under paragraph (a), the regulatory agency shall also consider:</p>
          <p>(1) the similarity of the most recent previous violation and the violation to be penalized;</p>
          <p>(2) the time elapsed since the last violation; and</p>
          <p>(3) the response of the subject of the order to the most recent previous violation.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.06.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Request for hearing; hearing; and final order.</span></h2>
          <p>A request for hearing must be in writing, delivered to the regulatory agency by certified mail within 20 calendar days after the receipt of the order, and specifically state the reasons for seeking review of the order. The regulatory agency must initiate a hearing within 30 calendar days from the date of receipt of the written request for hearing. The hearing shall be conducted pursuant to the contested case procedures in sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/14.57">14.57</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/14.62">14.62</a>. No earlier than ten calendar days after and within 30 calendar days of receipt of the presiding administrative law judge's report, the regulatory agency shall, based on all relevant facts, issue a final order modifying, vacating, or making the original order permanent. If, within 20 calendar days of receipt of the original order, the person that is the subject of the order fails to request a hearing in writing, the order becomes the final order of the regulatory agency.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.06.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Review of final order and payment of penalty.</span></h2>
          <p>Once the regulatory agency issues a final order, any penalty due under that order shall be paid within 30 calendar days after the date of the final order, unless review of the final order is requested. The final order of the regulatory agency may be appealed in the manner prescribed in sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/14.63">14.63</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/14.69">14.69</a>. If the final order is reviewed and upheld, the penalty shall be paid 30 calendar days after the date of the decision of the reviewing court. Failure to request an administrative hearing pursuant to subdivision 6 shall constitute a waiver of the right to further agency or judicial review of the final order.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.06.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Reinspections and effect of noncompliance.</span></h2>
          <p>If upon reinspection, or in the determination of the regulatory agency, it is found that any deficiency specified in the order has not been corrected or an acceptable corrective plan has not been developed, the person that is subject to the order is in noncompliance. The regulatory agency shall issue a notice of noncompliance and may impose any additional remedy available under this chapter.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.06.9">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9.<span class="headnote">Enforcement.</span></h2>
          <p>The attorney general may proceed on behalf of the regulatory agency to enforce penalties that are due and payable under this section in any manner provided by law for the collection of debts.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.06.10">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 10.<span class="headnote">Revocation; suspension; denial of license; permit; or registration.</span></h2>
          <p>Failure to pay penalties owed under this section constitutes grounds for the revocation or suspension of or refusal to reissue a license or permit issued by the regulatory agency under this chapter.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.06.11">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 11.<span class="headnote">Cumulative remedy.</span></h2>
          <p>The authority of the regulatory agency to issue an administrative penalty order is in addition to other lawfully available remedies.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.06.12">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 12.<span class="headnote">Mediation.</span></h2>
          <p>In addition to review under subdivision 6, the regulatory agency is authorized to enter into mediation concerning an order issued under this section if the regulatory agency and the subject of that order agree to mediation.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.149A.06.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/215/#laws.0.10.0">1997 c 215 s 10</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/0/171/#laws.0.13.0">2001 c 171 s 13</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/112/#laws.2.50.0">2003 c 112 art 2 s 50</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/101/#laws.2.109.0">2009 c 101 art 2 s 109</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.149A.07">
        <h1 class="shn">149A.07 INJUNCTIVE RELIEF.</h1>
        <p>In addition to any other remedy provided by law, the regulatory agency may bring an action for injunctive relief in any district court in any district in Minnesota to restrain any person from violation or threatened violation of any law, rule, order, stipulation agreement, settlement, compliance agreement, license, or permit which the regulatory agency is empowered to enforce, regulate, or issue.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.149A.07.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/215/#laws.0.11.0">1997 c 215 s 11</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/0/171/#laws.0.13.0">2001 c 171 s 13</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.149A.08">
        <h1 class="shn">149A.08 CEASE AND DESIST ORDER.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.08.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Authorization.</span></h2>
          <p>In addition to any other remedy provided by law, the regulatory agency may issue a cease and desist order to:</p>
          <p>(1) stop a person from violating or threatening to violate any law, rule, order, stipulation agreement, settlement, compliance agreement, license, or permit which the regulatory agency is empowered to regulate, enforce, or issue; or</p>
          <p>(2) prohibit a funeral provider from engaging in the sale of preneed funeral goods, funeral services, burial site goods, or burial site services if the funeral provider has been found in violation of any provision of this chapter.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.08.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Contents of order.</span></h2>
          <p>The cease and desist order must be in writing, state the specific reason for its issuance, and give notice of the right to request a hearing under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/14.57">14.57</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/14.62">14.62</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.08.2a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2a.<span class="headnote">Sale of preneed goods or services; contents of order, hearing, when effective.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) This subdivision applies to cease and desist orders issued pursuant to subdivision 1, clause (2).</p>
          <p>(b) In addition to the requirements of subdivision 2, a cease and desist order must also specify that the hearing to which the funeral provider has a right occurs, if requested, before the order goes into effect and that a timely request for a hearing automatically stays the cease and desist order.</p>
          <p>(c) A request for a hearing must be in writing, must be delivered to the regulatory agency by certified mail within 20 calendar days after the funeral provider receives the order, and must specifically state the reasons for seeking review of the order. If the funeral provider fails to request a hearing in writing within 20 calendar days of receipt of the order, the cease and desist order becomes the final order of the regulatory agency. If a funeral provider makes a timely request for a hearing, the cease and desist order is automatically stayed pending the outcome of the hearing. The regulatory agency must initiate a hearing within 30 calendar days from the date of receiving the written request for hearing. The hearing shall be conducted pursuant to sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/14.57">14.57</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/14.62">14.62</a>. No earlier than ten calendar days but within 30 calendar days of receiving the presiding administrative law judge's report, the regulatory agency shall issue a final order modifying, vacating, or making permanent the cease and desist order as the facts require.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.08.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Request for hearing; hearing; and final order.</span></h2>
          <p>This subdivision applies to cease and desist orders issued pursuant to subdivision 1, clause (1). A request for hearing must be in writing, delivered to the regulatory agency by certified mail within 20 calendar days after the receipt of the cease and desist order, and specifically state the reasons for seeking review of the order. The regulatory agency must initiate a hearing within 30 calendar days from the date of receipt of the written request for hearing. The hearing shall be conducted pursuant to sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/14.57">14.57</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/14.62">14.62</a>. No earlier than ten calendar days but within 30 calendar days of receipt of the presiding administrative law judge's report, the regulatory agency shall issue a final order modifying, vacating, or making permanent the cease and desist order as the facts require. If, within 20 calendar days of receipt of the cease and desist order, the subject of the order fails to request a hearing in writing, the cease and desist order becomes the final order of the regulatory agency.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.08.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Request for stay.</span></h2>
          <p>This subdivision applies to cease and desist orders issued pursuant to subdivision 1, clause (1). When a request for a stay accompanies a timely hearing request, the regulatory agency may, in the regulatory agency's discretion, grant the stay. If the regulatory agency does not grant a requested stay, the regulatory agency shall refer the request to the Office of Administrative Hearings within three working days from the receipt of the request. Within ten calendar days after receiving the request from the regulatory agency, an administrative law judge shall issue a recommendation to grant or deny the stay. The regulatory agency shall grant or deny the stay within five calendar days of receiving the administrative law judge's recommendation.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.08.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Review of final order.</span></h2>
          <p>A judicial review of the final order issued by the regulatory agency may be requested in the manner prescribed in sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/14.63">14.63</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/14.69">14.69</a>. Failure to request a hearing pursuant to subdivision 3 shall constitute a waiver of the right to further agency or judicial review of the final order.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.08.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Effect of noncompliance with order.</span></h2>
          <p>In the event of noncompliance with the cease and desist order, the regulatory agency may:</p>
          <p>(1) institute a proceeding in any district court in any district in Minnesota to obtain injunctive relief;</p>
          <p>(2) impose an administrative penalty, pursuant to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/149A.06">149A.06</a>, for each separate violation; or</p>
          <p>(3) revoke, suspend, limit, or condition the license or permit issued to the subject of the cease and desist order.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.08.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Additional remedies.</span></h2>
          <p>The issuance of a cease and desist order or injunctive relief does not relieve a person subject to regulation under this chapter from criminal prosecution by a competent authority.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.149A.08.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/215/#laws.0.12.0">1997 c 215 s 12</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2000/0/438/#laws.0.5.0">2000 c 438 s 5</a>-8; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/0/171/#laws.0.13.0">2001 c 171 s 13</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.149A.09">
        <h1 class="shn">149A.09 DENIAL; REFUSAL TO REISSUE; REVOCATION; SUSPENSION; LIMITATION OF LICENSE, REGISTRATION, OR PERMIT.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.09.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Denial; refusal to renew; revocation; and suspension.</span></h2>
          <p>The regulatory agency may deny, refuse to renew, revoke, or suspend any license, registration, or permit applied for or issued pursuant to this chapter when the person subject to regulation under this chapter:</p>
          <p>(1) does not meet or fails to maintain the minimum qualification for holding a license, registration, or permit under this chapter;</p>
          <p>(2) submits false or misleading material information to the regulatory agency in connection with a license, registration, or permit issued by the regulatory agency or the application for a license, registration, or permit;</p>
          <p>(3) violates any law, rule, order, stipulation agreement, settlement, compliance agreement, license, registration, or permit that regulates the removal, preparation, transportation, arrangements for disposition, or final disposition of dead human bodies in Minnesota or any other state in the United States;</p>
          <p>(4) is convicted of a crime, including a finding or verdict of guilt, an admission of guilt, or a no contest plea in any court in Minnesota or any other jurisdiction in the United States. "Conviction," as used in this subdivision, includes a conviction for an offense which, if committed in this state, would be deemed a felony or gross misdemeanor without regard to its designation elsewhere, or a criminal proceeding where a finding or verdict of guilty is made or returned, but the adjudication of guilt is either withheld or not entered;</p>
          <p>(5) is convicted of a crime, including a finding or verdict of guilt, an admission of guilt, or a no contest plea in any court in Minnesota or any other jurisdiction in the United States that the regulatory agency determines is reasonably related to the removal, preparation, transportation, arrangements for disposition or final disposition of dead human bodies, or the practice of mortuary science;</p>
          <p>(6) is adjudicated as mentally incompetent, mentally ill, developmentally disabled, or mentally ill and dangerous to the public;</p>
          <p>(7) has a conservator or guardian appointed;</p>
          <p>(8) fails to comply with an order issued by the regulatory agency or fails to pay an administrative penalty imposed by the regulatory agency;</p>
          <p>(9) owes uncontested delinquent taxes in the amount of $500 or more to the Minnesota Department of Revenue, or any other governmental agency authorized to collect taxes anywhere in the United States;</p>
          <p>(10) is in arrears on any court ordered family or child support obligations; or</p>
          <p>(11) engages in any conduct that, in the determination of the regulatory agency, is unprofessional as prescribed in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/149A.70#stat.149A.70.7">149A.70, subdivision 7</a>, or renders the person unfit to practice mortuary science or to operate a funeral establishment or crematory.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.09.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Hearings related to refusal to renew, suspension, or revocation of license, registration, or permit.</span></h2>
          <p>If the regulatory agency proposes to deny renewal, suspend, or revoke a license, registration, or permit issued under this chapter, the regulatory agency must first notify, in writing, the person against whom the action is proposed to be taken and provide an opportunity to request a hearing under the contested case provisions of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/14.57">14.57</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/14.62">14.62</a>. If the subject of the proposed action does not request a hearing by notifying the regulatory agency, by mail, within 20 calendar days after the receipt of the notice of proposed action, the regulatory agency may proceed with the action without a hearing and the action will be the final order of the regulatory agency.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.09.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Review of final order.</span></h2>
          <p>A judicial review of the final order issued by the regulatory agency may be requested in the manner prescribed in sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/14.63">14.63</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/14.69">14.69</a>. Failure to request a hearing pursuant to subdivision 2 shall constitute a waiver of the right to further agency or judicial review of the final order.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.09.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Limitations or qualifications placed on license, registration, or permit.</span></h2>
          <p>The regulatory agency may, where the facts support such action, place reasonable limitations or qualifications on the right to practice mortuary science, to operate a funeral establishment or crematory, or to perform activities or actions permitted under this chapter.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.09.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Restoring license, registration, or permit.</span></h2>
          <p>The regulatory agency may, where there is sufficient reason, restore a license, registration, or permit that has been revoked, reduce a period of suspension, or remove limitations or qualifications.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.149A.09.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/215/#laws.0.13.0">1997 c 215 s 13</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/0/171/#laws.0.13.0">2001 c 171 s 13</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/0/56/#laws.0.1.0">2005 c 56 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/127/#laws.18.5.0">2024 c 127 art 18 s 5</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.149A.10">
        <h1 class="shn">149A.10 ADDITIONAL REMEDIES.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.10.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Reimbursement of costs.</span></h2>
          <p>The regulatory agency may impose a fee on any person subject to regulation under this chapter to reimburse the Department of Health for all or part of the cost of contested case proceedings or civil action resulting in disciplinary action, including, but not limited to, the amount paid by the regulatory agency for services from the Office of Administrative Hearings, attorney fees, court reports, witnesses, reproduction of records, staff time, and other expenses.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.10.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Contested case and civil action; award of fees and expenses.</span></h2>
          <p>In a contested case proceeding or a civil action, the prevailing party other than the state, upon a showing that the position of the state was not substantially justified, shall be awarded fees and other expenses pursuant to sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/14.62#stat.14.62.3">14.62, subdivision 3</a>; and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/15.471">15.471</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/15.474">15.474</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.10.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Other actions.</span></h2>
          <p>The regulatory agency may take any other lawful action justified by the facts of the case.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.149A.10.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/215/#laws.0.14.0">1997 c 215 s 14</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/0/171/#laws.0.13.0">2001 c 171 s 13</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.149A.11">
        <h1 class="shn">149A.11 PUBLICATION OF DISCIPLINARY ACTIONS.</h1>
        <p>The regulatory agencies shall report all disciplinary measures or actions taken to the commissioner. At least annually, the commissioner shall publish and make available to the public a description of all disciplinary measures or actions taken by the regulatory agencies. The publication shall include, for each disciplinary measure or action taken, the name and business address of the licensee, intern, or transfer care specialist; the nature of the misconduct; and the measure or action taken by the regulatory agency.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.149A.11.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/215/#laws.0.15.0">1997 c 215 s 15</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/0/171/#laws.0.8.0">2001 c 171 s 8</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/127/#laws.18.6.0">2024 c 127 art 18 s 6</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.149A.20">
        <h1 class="shn">149A.20 LICENSE TO PRACTICE MORTUARY SCIENCE.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.20.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">License required.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">Except as provided in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/149A.01#stat.149A.01.3">149A.01, subdivision 3</a>, any person who takes charge of or removes from the place of death a dead human body, or prepares a dead human body for final disposition in any manner, or arranges, directs, or supervises a funeral, memorial service, or graveside service must possess a valid license to practice mortuary science issued by the commissioner. A funeral establishment may provide a nonlicensed individual to direct or supervise a memorial service provided they disclose that information to the person or persons with the authority to make the funeral arrangement as provided in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/149A.80">149A.80</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.20.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Effective date.</span></h2>
          <p>The requirements in subdivisions 3 to 13 for initial licensure in mortuary science are effective on July 1, 1997, except as provided in this chapter.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.20.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Age requirement.</span></h2>
          <p>The person must be at least 21 years of age.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.20.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Educational requirements.</span></h2>
          <p class="in"> The person shall have:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) received a bachelor of science degree with a major in mortuary science from an accredited college or university;</p>
          <p class="in">(2) received a bachelor of science or arts degree from an accredited college or university and completed a separate course of study in mortuary science from a college of funeral service education accredited by the American Board of Funeral Service Education; or</p>
