          <p class="in">(3) completed credit hours at accredited colleges or universities that in the numerical aggregate and distribution are the functional equivalent of a bachelor of arts or science degree and have completed a separate course of study in mortuary science from a program of mortuary science accredited by the American Board of Funeral Service Education.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.20.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Examinations.</span></h2>
          <p>After having met the educational requirements of subdivision 4, a person must attain a passing score on the National Board Examination administered by the Conference of Funeral Service Examining Boards of the United States, Inc. or any other examination that, in the determination of the commissioner, adequately and accurately assesses the knowledge and skills required to practice mortuary science. In addition, a person must attain a passing score on the state licensing examination administered by or on behalf of the commissioner. The state examination shall encompass the laws and rules of Minnesota that pertain to the practice of mortuary science. The commissioner shall make available copies of all pertinent laws and rules prior to administration of the state licensing examination. If a passing score is not attained on the state examination, the individual must wait two weeks before they can retake the examination.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.20.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Internship.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) A person who attains a passing score on both examinations in subdivision 5 must complete a registered internship under the direct supervision of an individual currently licensed to practice mortuary science in Minnesota. Interns must file with the commissioner:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) the appropriate fee; and</p>
          <p class="in">(2) a registration form indicating the name and home address of the intern, the date the internship begins, and the name, license number, and business address of the supervising mortuary science licensee.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) Any changes in information provided in the registration must be immediately reported to the commissioner. The internship shall be a minimum of 2,080 hours to be completed within a three-year period, however, the commissioner may waive up to 520 hours of the internship time requirement upon satisfactory completion of a clinical or practicum in mortuary science administered through the program of mortuary science of the University of Minnesota or a substantially similar program approved by the commissioner. Registrations must be renewed on an annual basis if they exceed one calendar year. During the internship period, the intern must be under the direct supervision of a person holding a current license to practice mortuary science in Minnesota. An intern may be registered under only one licensee at any given time and may be directed and supervised only by the registered licensee. The registered licensee shall have only one intern registered at any given time. The commissioner shall issue to each registered intern a registration permit that must be displayed with the other establishment and practice licenses. While under the direct supervision of the licensee, the intern must complete 25 case reports in each of the following areas: embalming, funeral arrangements, and services. Case reports, on forms provided by the commissioner, shall be completed by the intern and filed with the commissioner prior to the completion of the internship. Information contained in these reports that identifies the subject or the family of the subject embalmed or the subject or the family of the subject of the funeral shall be classified as licensing data under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/13.41#stat.13.41.2">13.41, subdivision 2</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.20.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Application procedure and documentation.</span></h2>
          <p>After completing the registered internship, the applicant for an initial license to practice mortuary science must submit to the commissioner a complete application and the appropriate fee. A complete application includes:</p>
          <p>(1) a completed application form, as provided by the commissioner;</p>
          <p>(2) proof of age;</p>
          <p>(3) an official transcript from each post high school educational institution attended, including colleges of funeral service education;</p>
          <p>(4) certification of a passing score on the National Board Examination from the commissioner of the Conference of Funeral Service Examining Boards of the United States, Inc.;</p>
          <p>(5) a copy of the notification of a passing score on the state licensing examination; and</p>
          <p>(6) a signed, dated, and notarized affidavit from the licensee who supervised the Minnesota internship stating the date the internship began and ended and that both the applicant and the supervising licensee fulfilled the requirements under subdivision 6.</p>
          <p>Upon receipt of the completed application and appropriate fee, the commissioner shall review and verify all information. Upon completion of the verification process and resolution of any deficiencies in the application information, the commissioner shall make a determination, based on all the information available, to grant or deny licensure. If the commissioner's determination is to grant licensure, the applicant shall be notified and the license shall issue and remain valid for a period prescribed on the license, but not to exceed one calendar year from the date of issuance of the license. If the commissioner's determination is to deny licensure, the commissioner must notify the applicant, in writing, of the denial and provide the specific reason for the denial.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.20.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/108/#laws.12.109.0">2013 c 108 art 12 s 109</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.20.9">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9.<span class="headnote">Period of licensure.</span></h2>
          <p>All licenses to practice mortuary science issued by the commissioner shall be valid for one calendar year, beginning on January 1 and ending on December 31 regardless of the date of issuance. Fees may not be prorated.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.20.10">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 10.<span class="headnote">Display of license.</span></h2>
          <p>Each license to practice mortuary science must be conspicuously displayed at all times in the holder's place of business. Conspicuous display means in a location where a member of the general public within the holder's place of business will be able to observe and read the license.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.20.11">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 11.<span class="headnote">Nontransferability of license.</span></h2>
          <p>A license to practice mortuary science is not assignable or transferable and is not valid for any person other than the individual named.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.20.12">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 12.<span class="headnote">Reporting changes in license information.</span></h2>
          <p>Any change of license information must be reported to the commissioner, on forms provided by the commissioner, no later than 30 calendar days after the change occurs. Failure to report changes is grounds for disciplinary action.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.20.13">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 13.<span class="headnote">Application information.</span></h2>
          <p>All information submitted to the commissioner by an applicant for licensure to practice mortuary science is classified as licensing data under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/13.41">13.41</a>, with the exception of the name and address of the applicant. Upon issuance of a license to practice mortuary science, all application information becomes licensing data under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/13.41#stat.13.41.5">13.41, subdivision 5</a>, with the exception of internship case report data as classified under subdivision 6.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.149A.20.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/215/#laws.0.16.0">1997 c 215 s 16</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/112/#laws.2.50.0">2003 c 112 art 2 s 50</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/114/#laws.0.15.0">2007 c 114 s 15</a>-17; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/101/#laws.2.109.0">2009 c 101 art 2 s 109</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2015/0/71/#laws.8.51.0">2015 c 71 art 8 s 51</a>,52</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.149A.30">
        <h1 class="shn">149A.30 RECIPROCAL LICENSING.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.30.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Licensees of other states.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner may issue a license to practice mortuary science to a person who holds a current license or other credential from another jurisdiction if the commissioner determines that the requirements for that license or other credential are substantially similar to the requirements under this chapter. The individual seeking reciprocal licensing must:</p>
          <p>(1) attain a passing score on the Minnesota state licensing examination;</p>
          <p>(2) submit to the commissioner the documentation described in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/149A.20#stat.149A.20.7">149A.20, subdivision 7</a>, clauses (1) and (5); and</p>
          <p>(3) pay the appropriate licensing fee.</p>
          <p>When, in the determination of the commissioner, all of the requirements of this subdivision have been met, the commissioner shall, based on all the information available, grant or deny licensure. If the commissioner grants licensure, the applicant shall be notified and the license shall issue and remain valid for a period prescribed on the license, but not to exceed one calendar year from the date of issuance of the license. If the commissioner denies licensure, the commissioner must notify the applicant, in writing, of the denial and provide the specific reason for denial.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.30.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/108/#laws.12.109.0">2013 c 108 art 12 s 109</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.30.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Period of licensure.</span></h2>
          <p>All reciprocal licenses to practice mortuary science issued by the commissioner shall be valid for one calendar year, beginning on January 1 and ending on December 31 regardless of the date of issuance. Fees shall not be prorated.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.30.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Display of license.</span></h2>
          <p>Each reciprocal license to practice mortuary science must be conspicuously displayed at all times in the holder's place of business. Conspicuous display means in a location where a member of the general public within the holder's place of business will be able to observe and read the license.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.30.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Nontransferability of license.</span></h2>
          <p>A reciprocal license to practice mortuary science is not assignable or transferable and shall not be valid for any person other than the person named.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.30.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Reporting changes in license information.</span></h2>
          <p>Any change of license information must be reported to the commissioner, on forms provided by the commissioner, no later than 30 calendar days after the change occurs. Failure to report changes is grounds for disciplinary action.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.30.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Application information.</span></h2>
          <p>All information submitted to the commissioner by an applicant for reciprocal licensure is classified as licensing data under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/13.41#stat.13.41.2">13.41, subdivision 2</a>, with the exception of the name and address of the applicant. Upon issuance of a license to practice mortuary science, all application information becomes licensing data under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/13.41#stat.13.41.5">13.41, subdivision 5</a>.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.149A.30.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/215/#laws.0.17.0">1997 c 215 s 17</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/112/#laws.2.50.0">2003 c 112 art 2 s 50</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/101/#laws.2.109.0">2009 c 101 art 2 s 109</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.149A.40">
        <h1 class="shn">149A.40 RENEWAL OF LICENSE TO PRACTICE MORTUARY SCIENCE.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.40.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Renewal request.</span></h2>
          <p>All licenses to practice mortuary science issued by the commissioner expire on December 31 of the calendar year in which the license is issued and must be renewed to remain valid.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.40.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Limited license; funeral director only.</span></h2>
          <p>Any person who held a funeral director only license on July 31, 1957, may renew the license under this section. Individuals practicing under a funeral director only license issued under this subdivision are prohibited from engaging in the practice of embalming a dead human body.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.40.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Renewal procedure and documentation.</span></h2>
          <p>Licensees who wish to renew their licenses must submit to the commissioner a completed renewal application and the renewal fee no later than December 31 of the year in which the license was issued. A completed renewal application includes:</p>
          <p>(1) a completed renewal application form, as provided by the commissioner; and</p>
          <p>(2) the appropriate renewal licensing fee.</p>
          <p>Upon receipt of the completed renewal application and appropriate fee, the commissioner shall review and verify all information. Upon completion of the verification process and resolution of any deficiencies in the renewal application information, the commissioner shall make a determination, based on all the information available, to reissue or refuse to reissue the license. If the commissioner's determination is to reissue the license, the applicant shall be notified and the license shall issue and remain valid for a period prescribed on the license, but not to exceed one calendar year from the date of issuance of the license. If the commissioner's determination is to refuse to reissue the license, section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/149A.09#stat.149A.09.2">149A.09, subdivision 2</a>, applies.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.40.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Penalty for late filing.</span></h2>
          <p>Renewal applications received after the expiration date of a license shall result in the assessment of a late filing penalty. The late filing penalty must be paid before the reissuance of the license and received by the commissioner no later than 31 calendar days after the expiration date of the license.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.40.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Lapse of license.</span></h2>
          <p>A license to practice mortuary science shall automatically lapse when a completed renewal application and renewal fee are not received by the commissioner within 31 calendar days after the expiration date of a license or a late filing penalty assessed under subdivision 4 is not received by the commissioner within 31 calendar days after the expiration of a license.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.40.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Effect of lapse of license.</span></h2>
          <p>Upon the lapse of a license, the person to whom the license was issued is no longer licensed to practice mortuary science in Minnesota. The commissioner shall issue a cease and desist order to prevent the individual from engaging in the practice of mortuary science in Minnesota and may pursue any additional lawful remedies as justified by the case.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.40.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Restoration of lapsed license.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner may restore a lapsed license upon receipt and review of a completed renewal application, renewal fee, and late filing penalty, provided that the receipt is made within one calendar year from the expiration date of the lapsed license and the person has not violated the cease and desist order issued by the commissioner. If a lapsed license is not restored within one calendar year from the expiration date of the lapsed license, the person to whom the lapsed license was issued cannot be relicensed until the requirements in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/149A.20">149A.20</a> are met.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.40.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/108/#laws.12.109.0">2013 c 108 art 12 s 109</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.40.9">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9.<span class="headnote">Reporting changes in license information.</span></h2>
          <p>Any change of license information must be reported to the commissioner, on forms provided by the commissioner, no later than 30 calendar days after the change occurs. Failure to report changes is grounds for disciplinary action.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.40.10">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 10.<span class="headnote">Application information.</span></h2>
          <p>All information submitted to the commissioner by an applicant for renewal of licensure to practice mortuary science is classified as licensing data under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/13.41#stat.13.41.2">13.41, subdivision 2</a>, with the exception of the name and address of the applicant. Upon reissuance of a license to practice mortuary science, all application information becomes licensing data under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/13.41#stat.13.41.5">13.41, subdivision 5</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.40.11">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 11.<span class="headnote">Continuing education.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">The commissioner shall require 15 continuing education hours for renewal of a license to practice mortuary science. Nine of the hours must be in the following areas: body preparation, care, or handling, 3 CE hours; professional practices, 3 CE hours; and regulation and ethics, 3 CE hours. Continuing education hours shall be reported to the commissioner every other year based on the licensee's license number. Licensees whose license ends in an odd number must report CE hours at renewal time every odd year. If a licensee's license ends in an even number, the licensee must report the licensee's CE hours at renewal time every even year.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.149A.40.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/215/#laws.0.18.0">1997 c 215 s 18</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/112/#laws.2.50.0">2003 c 112 art 2 s 50</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/114/#laws.0.18.0">2007 c 114 s 18</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/101/#laws.2.109.0">2009 c 101 art 2 s 109</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2015/0/71/#laws.8.53.0">2015 c 71 art 8 s 53</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.149A.45">
        <h1 class="shn">149A.45 EMERITUS REGISTRATION FOR MORTUARY SCIENCE PRACTITIONERS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.45.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Application.</span></h2>
          <p>Any mortuary science practitioner licensed to practice mortuary science in Minnesota under this chapter may apply to the commissioner for mortuary science practitioner emeritus registration if the person declares that he or she is retired in all jurisdictions from the active practice of mortuary science and if the person is not subject to any disciplinary action by the commissioner and not subject to an order by the commissioner imposing a suspended, conditional, or restricted license to practice mortuary science. For purposes of this section, a person is retired if the person has completely ceased the active practice of mortuary science in all jurisdictions for any reason. The mortuary science practitioner may apply to the commissioner using the practitioner's licensure form or by petitioning the commissioner.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.45.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Status of registrant.</span></h2>
          <p>An emeritus registration is not a license to engage in the practice of mortuary science. A person registered under this section shall not engage in the practice of mortuary science.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.45.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Continuing education requirements.</span></h2>
          <p>The continuing education requirements that apply to mortuary science practitioners shall not apply to a person registered under this section.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.45.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Documentation of status.</span></h2>
          <p>A person granted emeritus registration shall, upon payment of a $50 fee, be issued a certificate by the commissioner certifying that the person has received emeritus registration and has completed his or her active professional career licensed in good standing with the commissioner. The $50 fee shall be a onetime fee.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.45.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Renewal cycle or fee.</span></h2>
          <p>A person registered under this section shall not be subject to a registration renewal cycle or any renewal fees.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.45.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/108/#laws.12.109.0">2013 c 108 art 12 s 109</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.45.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Reinstatement.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">After one year a person who registers under this section may reapply meeting current requirements for licensure listed in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/149A.20">149A.20</a>.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.149A.45.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2002/0/399/#laws.0.1.0">2002 c 399 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/114/#laws.0.19.0">2007 c 114 s 19</a>,20; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/101/#laws.2.109.0">2009 c 101 art 2 s 109</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.149A.47">
        <h1 class="shn">149A.47 TRANSFER CARE SPECIALIST.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.47.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">General.</span></h2>
          <p>A transfer care specialist may remove a dead human body from the place of death under the direct supervision of a licensed mortician if the transfer care specialist is registered with the commissioner in accordance with this section. A transfer care specialist is not licensed to engage in the practice of mortuary science and shall not engage in the practice of mortuary science except as provided in this section. A transfer care specialist must be an employee of a licensed funeral establishment.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.47.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Registration.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) To be eligible for registration as a transfer care specialist, an applicant must submit to the commissioner:</p>
          <p>(1) a completed application on a form provided by the commissioner that includes at a minimum:</p>
          <p>(i) the applicant's name, home address and telephone number, business name, business address and telephone number, and email address; and</p>
          <p>(ii) the name, license number, business name, and business address and telephone number of the supervising licensed mortician;</p>
          <p>(2) proof of completion of a training program that meets the requirements specified in subdivision 4; and</p>
          <p>(3) the appropriate fee specified in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/149A.65">149A.65</a>.</p>
          <p>(b) All transfer care specialist registrations are valid for one calendar year, beginning on January 1 and ending on December 31 regardless of the date of issuance. Fees shall not be prorated.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.47.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Duties.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A transfer care specialist registered under this section is authorized to perform the removal of a dead human body from the place of death in accordance with this chapter to a licensed funeral establishment. A transfer care specialist must comply with the universal precaution requirements in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/149A.91#stat.149A.91.1">149A.91, subdivision 1</a>, when handling a dead human body.</p>
          <p>(b) A transfer care specialist must work under the direct supervision of a licensed mortician. The supervising mortician is responsible for the work performed by the transfer care specialist. A licensed mortician may supervise up to four transfer care specialists at any one time.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.47.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Training program and continuing education.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Each transfer care specialist must complete a training program prior to initial registration. A training program must be at least seven hours long and must cover, at a minimum, the following:</p>
          <p>(1) ethical care and transportation procedures for a deceased person;</p>
          <p>(2) health and safety concerns to the public and the individual performing the transfer of the deceased person, and the use of universal precautions and other reasonable precautions to minimize the risk for transmitting communicable diseases; and</p>
          <p>(3) all relevant state and federal laws and regulations related to the transfer and transportation of deceased persons.</p>
          <p>(b) A transfer care specialist must complete three hours of continuing education annually on content described in paragraph (a), clauses (1) to (3), and submit evidence of completion with the individual's registration renewal.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.47.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Renewal.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A registration issued under this section expires on December 31 of the calendar year in which the registration was issued and must be renewed to remain valid.</p>
          <p>(b) To renew a registration, a transfer care specialist must submit to the commissioner a completed renewal application as provided by the commissioner and the appropriate fee specified in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/149A.65">149A.65</a>. The renewal application must include proof of completion of the continuing education requirements in subdivision 4.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.149A.47.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/127/#laws.18.7.0">2024 c 127 art 18 s 7</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.149A.50">
        <h1 class="shn">149A.50 LICENSE TO OPERATE A FUNERAL ESTABLISHMENT.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.50.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">License required.</span></h2>
          <p>Except as provided in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/149A.01#stat.149A.01.3">149A.01, subdivision 3</a>, no person shall maintain, manage, or operate a place or premise devoted to or used in the holding, care, or preparation of a dead human body for final disposition, or any place used as the office or place of business for the provision of funeral services, without possessing a valid license to operate a funeral establishment issued by the commissioner of health.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.50.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Requirements for funeral establishment.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">A funeral establishment licensed under this section must:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) comply with preparation and embalming room requirements as described in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/149A.92">149A.92</a>;</p>
          <p class="in">(2) contain office space for making arrangements; and</p>
          <p class="in">(3) comply with applicable local and state building codes, zoning laws, and ordinances.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.50.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Application; procedure; documentation; initial inspection.</span></h2>
          <p>An applicant for a license to operate a funeral establishment shall submit to the commissioner a completed application and the appropriate fees. A completed application includes:</p>
          <p>(1) a completed application form, as provided by the commissioner;</p>
          <p>(2) proof of business form and ownership; and</p>
          <p>(3) proof of liability insurance coverage or other financial documentation, as determined by the commissioner, that demonstrates the applicant's ability to respond in damages for liability arising from the ownership, maintenance, management, or operation of a funeral establishment.</p>
          <p>Upon receipt of the application and appropriate fee, the commissioner shall review and verify all information. Upon completion of the verification process and resolution of any deficiencies in the application information, the commissioner shall conduct an initial inspection of the premises to be licensed. After the inspection and resolution of any deficiencies found, and any reinspections as may be necessary, the commissioner shall make a determination, based on all the information available, to grant or deny licensure. If the commissioner's determination is to grant the license, the applicant shall be notified and the license shall issue and remain valid for a period prescribed on the license, but not to exceed one calendar year from the date of issuance of the license. If the commissioner's determination is to deny the license, the commissioner must notify the applicant, in writing, of the denial and provide the specific reason for denial.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.50.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Nontransferability of license.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">A license to operate a funeral establishment is not assignable or transferable and shall not be valid for any person other than the one named. Each license issued to operate a funeral establishment is valid only for the location identified on the license. A 50 percent or more change in ownership or location of the funeral establishment automatically terminates the license. Separate licenses shall be required of two or more persons or other legal entities operating from the same location.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.50.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Display of license.</span></h2>
          <p>Each license to operate a funeral establishment must be conspicuously displayed in the funeral establishment at all times. Conspicuous display means in a location where a member of the general public within the funeral establishment will be able to observe and read the license.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.50.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/108/#laws.12.109.0">2013 c 108 art 12 s 109</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.50.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Period of licensure.</span></h2>
          <p>All licenses to operate a funeral establishment issued by the commissioner are valid for a period of one calendar year beginning on July 1 and ending on June 30, regardless of the date of issuance. Fees shall not be prorated.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.50.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Reporting changes in license information.</span></h2>
          <p>Any change of license information must be reported to the commissioner, on forms provided by the commissioner, no later than 30 calendar days after the change occurs. Failure to report changes is grounds for disciplinary action.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.50.9">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9.<span class="headnote">Application information.</span></h2>
          <p>All information submitted to the commissioner by an applicant for a license to operate a funeral establishment is classified as licensing data under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/13.41#stat.13.41.5">13.41, subdivision 5</a>.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.149A.50.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/215/#laws.0.19.0">1997 c 215 s 19</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/112/#laws.2.50.0">2003 c 112 art 2 s 50</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/114/#laws.0.21.0">2007 c 114 s 21</a>,22; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/101/#laws.2.109.0">2009 c 101 art 2 s 109</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2016/0/189/#laws.20.22.0">2016 c 189 art 20 s 22</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.149A.51">
        <h1 class="shn">149A.51 RENEWAL OF LICENSE TO OPERATE A FUNERAL ESTABLISHMENT.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.51.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Renewal required.</span></h2>
          <p>A license to operate a funeral establishment issued by the commissioner expires on June 30 following the date of issuance of the license and must be renewed to remain valid.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.51.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Renewal procedure and documentation.</span></h2>
          <p>Licensees who wish to renew their licenses must submit to the commissioner a completed renewal application and the renewal fee no later than June 30 following the date the license was issued. A completed renewal application includes:</p>
          <p>(1) a completed renewal application form, as provided by the commissioner; and</p>
          <p>(2) proof of liability insurance coverage or other financial documentation, as determined by the commissioner, that demonstrates the applicant's ability to respond in damages for liability arising from the ownership, maintenance, management, or operation of a funeral establishment.</p>
          <p>Upon receipt of the completed renewal application and appropriate fee, the commissioner shall review and verify all information. Upon completion of the verification process and resolution of any deficiencies in the renewal application information, the commissioner shall make a determination, based on all the information available, to reissue or refuse to reissue the license. If the commissioner's determination is to reissue the license, the applicant shall be notified and the license shall issue and remain valid for a period prescribed on the license, but not to exceed one calendar year from the date of issuance of the license. If the commissioner's determination is to refuse to reissue the license, section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/149A.09#stat.149A.09.2">149A.09, subdivision 2</a>, applies.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.51.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Penalty for late filing.</span></h2>
          <p>Renewal applications received after the expiration date of a license shall result in the assessment of a late filing penalty. The late filing penalty must be paid prior to the reissuance of the license and received by the commissioner no later than 31 calendar days after the expiration date of the license.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.51.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Lapse of license.</span></h2>
          <p>Licenses to operate funeral establishments shall automatically lapse when a completed renewal application and renewal fee are not received by the commissioner within 31 calendar days after the expiration date of a license or a late filing penalty assessed under subdivision 3 is not received by the commissioner within 31 calendar days after the expiration of a license.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.51.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Effect of lapse of license.</span></h2>
          <p>Upon the lapse of a license, the person to whom the license was issued is no longer licensed to operate a funeral establishment in Minnesota. The commissioner shall issue a cease and desist order to prevent the holder of a lapsed license from operating a funeral establishment in Minnesota and may pursue any additional lawful remedies as justified by the case.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.51.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Restoration of lapsed license.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner may restore a lapsed license upon receipt and review of a completed renewal application, receipt of the renewal fee and late filing penalty, reinspection of the premises, and receipt of the reinspection fee, provided that the receipt is made within one calendar year from the expiration date of the lapsed license, and the cease and desist order issued by the commissioner has not been violated. If a lapsed license is not restored within one calendar year from the expiration date of the lapsed license, the holder of the lapsed license may not be relicensed until the requirements in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/149A.50">149A.50</a> are met.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.51.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/108/#laws.12.109.0">2013 c 108 art 12 s 109</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.51.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Reporting changes in license information.</span></h2>
          <p>Any change of license information must be reported to the commissioner, on forms provided by the commissioner, no later than 30 calendar days after the change occurs. Failure to report changes is grounds for disciplinary action.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.51.9">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9.<span class="headnote">Application information.</span></h2>
          <p>All information submitted to the commissioner by an applicant for renewal of licensure to operate a funeral establishment is classified as licensing data under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/13.41#stat.13.41.5">13.41, subdivision 5</a>.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.149A.51.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/215/#laws.0.20.0">1997 c 215 s 20</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/112/#laws.2.50.0">2003 c 112 art 2 s 50</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/101/#laws.2.109.0">2009 c 101 art 2 s 109</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.149A.52">
        <h1 class="shn">149A.52 LICENSE TO OPERATE A CREMATORY.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.52.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">License requirement.</span></h2>
          <p>Except as provided in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/149A.01#stat.149A.01.3">149A.01, subdivision 3</a>, no person shall maintain, manage, or operate a place or premises devoted to or used in the holding and cremation of a dead human body without possessing a valid license to operate a crematory issued by the commissioner of health.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.52.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Requirements for crematory.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A crematory licensed under this section must consist of:</p>
          <p>(1) a building or structure that complies with applicable local and state building codes, zoning laws and ordinances, and environmental standards, containing one or more cremation chambers or retorts for the cremation of dead human bodies;</p>
          <p>(2) a motorized mechanical device for grinding, crushing, or pulverizing the cremated remains to a granulated appearance appropriate for final disposition; and</p>
          <p>(3) an appropriate holding facility for dead human bodies awaiting cremation.</p>
          <p>(b) A crematory licensed under this section may also contain a display room for funeral goods.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.52.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Application procedure; documentation; initial inspection.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">An applicant for a license to operate a crematory shall submit to the commissioner a completed application. A completed application includes:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) a completed application form, as provided by the commissioner;</p>
          <p class="in">(2) proof of business form and ownership; and</p>
          <p class="in">(3) proof of liability insurance coverage or other financial documentation, as determined by the commissioner, that demonstrates the applicant's ability to respond in damages for liability arising from the ownership, maintenance, management, or operation of a crematory.</p>
          <p class="in">Upon receipt of the application and appropriate fee, the commissioner shall review and verify all information. Upon completion of the verification process and resolution of any deficiencies in the application information, the commissioner shall conduct an initial inspection of the premises to be licensed. After the inspection and resolution of any deficiencies found and any reinspections as may be necessary, the commissioner shall make a determination, based on all the information available, to grant or deny licensure. If the commissioner's determination is to grant the license, the applicant shall be notified and the license shall issue and remain valid for a period prescribed on the license, but not to exceed one calendar year from the date of issuance of the license. If the commissioner's determination is to deny the license, the commissioner must notify the applicant, in writing, of the denial and provide the specific reason for denial.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.52.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Nontransferability of license.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">A license to operate a crematory is not assignable or transferable and shall not be valid for any person other than the one named. Each license issued to operate a crematory is valid only for the location identified on the license. A 50 percent or more change in ownership or location of the crematory automatically terminates the license. Separate licenses shall be required of two or more persons or other legal entities operating from the same location.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.52.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Display of license.</span></h2>
          <p>Each license to operate a crematory must be conspicuously displayed in the crematory at all times. Conspicuous display means in a location where a member of the general public within the crematory will be able to observe and read the license.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.52.5a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5a.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/108/#laws.12.109.0">2013 c 108 art 12 s 109</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.52.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Period of licensure.</span></h2>
          <p>All licenses to operate a crematory issued by the commissioner are valid for a period of one calendar year beginning on July 1 and ending on June 30, regardless of the date of issuance.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.52.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Reporting changes in license information.</span></h2>
          <p>Any change of license information must be reported to the commissioner, on forms provided by the commissioner, no later than 30 calendar days after the change occurs. Failure to report changes is grounds for disciplinary action.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.52.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Application information.</span></h2>
          <p>All information submitted to the commissioner by an applicant for a license to operate a crematory is classified as licensing data under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/13.41#stat.13.41.5">13.41, subdivision 5</a>.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.149A.52.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/215/#laws.0.21.0">1997 c 215 s 21</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/114/#laws.0.23.0">2007 c 114 s 23</a>,24; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/147/#laws.9.31.0">2007 c 147 art 9 s 31</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/101/#laws.2.109.0">2009 c 101 art 2 s 109</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.149A.53">
        <h1 class="shn">149A.53 RENEWAL OF LICENSE TO OPERATE CREMATORY.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.53.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Renewal required.</span></h2>
          <p>All licenses to operate a crematory issued by the commissioner expire on June 30 following the date of issuance of the license and must be renewed to remain valid.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.53.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Renewal procedure and documentation.</span></h2>
          <p>Licensees who wish to renew their licenses must submit to the commissioner a completed renewal application no later than June 30 following the date the license was issued. A completed renewal application includes:</p>
          <p>(1) a completed renewal application form, as provided by the commissioner; and</p>
          <p>(2) proof of liability insurance coverage or other financial documentation, as determined by the commissioner, that demonstrates the applicant's ability to respond in damages for liability arising from the ownership, maintenance, management, or operation of a crematory.</p>
          <p>Upon receipt of the completed renewal application, the commissioner shall review and verify the information. Upon completion of the verification process and resolution of any deficiencies in the renewal application information, the commissioner shall make a determination, based on all the information available, to reissue or refuse to reissue the license. If the commissioner's determination is to reissue the license, the applicant shall be notified and the license shall issue and remain valid for a period prescribed on the license, but not to exceed one calendar year from the date of issuance of the license. If the commissioner's determination is to refuse to reissue the license, section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/149A.09#stat.149A.09.2">149A.09, subdivision 2</a>, applies.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.53.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Penalty for late filing.</span></h2>
          <p>Renewal applications received after the expiration date of a license will result in the assessment of a late filing penalty. The late filing penalty must be paid before the reissuance of the license and received by the commissioner no later than 31 calendar days after the expiration date of the license.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.53.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Lapse of license.</span></h2>
          <p>Licenses to operate crematories shall automatically lapse when a completed renewal application is not received by the commissioner within 31 calendar days after the expiration date of a license, or a late filing penalty assessed under subdivision 3 is not received by the commissioner within 31 calendar days after the expiration of a license.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.53.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Effect of lapse of license.</span></h2>
          <p>Upon the lapse of a license, the person to whom the license was issued is no longer licensed to operate a crematory in Minnesota. The commissioner shall issue a cease and desist order to prevent the lapsed license holder from operating a crematory in Minnesota and may pursue any additional lawful remedies as justified by the case.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.53.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Restoration of lapsed license.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner may restore a lapsed license upon receipt and review of a completed renewal application, receipt of the late filing penalty, and reinspection of the premises, provided that the receipt is made within one calendar year from the expiration date of the lapsed license and the cease and desist order issued by the commissioner has not been violated. If a lapsed license is not restored within one calendar year from the expiration date of the lapsed license, the holder of the lapsed license cannot be relicensed until the requirements in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/149A.52">149A.52</a> are met.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.53.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Reporting changes in license information.</span></h2>
          <p>Any change of license information must be reported to the commissioner, on forms provided by the commissioner, no later than 30 calendar days after the change occurs. Failure to report changes is grounds for disciplinary action.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.53.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Application information.</span></h2>
          <p>All information submitted to the commissioner by an applicant for renewal of licensure to operate a crematory is classified as licensing data under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/13.41#stat.13.41.5">13.41, subdivision 5</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.53.9">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/108/#laws.12.109.0">2013 c 108 art 12 s 109</a>]</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.149A.53.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/215/#laws.0.22.0">1997 c 215 s 22</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/114/#laws.0.25.0">2007 c 114 s 25</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/101/#laws.2.109.0">2009 c 101 art 2 s 109</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.149A.54">
        <h1 class="shn">149A.54 LICENSE TO OPERATE AN ALKALINE HYDROLYSIS FACILITY.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.54.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">License requirement.</span></h2>
          <p>Except as provided in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/149A.01">149A.01</a>, subdivision 3, a place or premise shall not be maintained, managed, or operated which is devoted to or used in the holding and alkaline hydrolysis of a dead human body without possessing a valid license to operate an alkaline hydrolysis facility issued by the commissioner of health.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.54.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Requirements for an alkaline hydrolysis facility.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) An alkaline hydrolysis facility licensed under this section must consist of:</p>
          <p>(1) a building or structure that complies with applicable local and state building codes, zoning laws and ordinances, and wastewater management and environmental standards, containing one or more alkaline hydrolysis vessels for the alkaline hydrolysis of dead human bodies;</p>
          <p>(2) a method approved by the commissioner of health to dry the hydrolyzed remains and which is located within the licensed facility;</p>
          <p>(3) a means approved by the commissioner of health for refrigeration of dead human bodies awaiting alkaline hydrolysis;</p>
          <p>(4) an appropriate means of processing hydrolyzed remains to a granulated appearance appropriate for final disposition; and</p>
          <p>(5) an appropriate holding facility for dead human bodies awaiting alkaline hydrolysis.</p>
          <p>(b) An alkaline hydrolysis facility licensed under this section may also contain a display room for funeral goods.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.54.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Application procedure; documentation; initial inspection.</span></h2>
          <p>An application to license and operate an alkaline hydrolysis facility shall be submitted to the commissioner of health. A completed application includes:</p>
          <p>(1) a completed application form, as provided by the commissioner;</p>
          <p>(2) proof of business form and ownership;</p>
          <p>(3) proof of liability insurance coverage or other financial documentation, as determined by the commissioner, that demonstrates the applicant's ability to respond in damages for liability arising from the ownership, maintenance management, or operation of an alkaline hydrolysis facility; and</p>
          <p>(4) copies of wastewater and other environmental regulatory permits and environmental regulatory licenses necessary to conduct operations.</p>
          <p class="bl">Upon receipt of the application and appropriate fee, the commissioner shall review and verify all information. Upon completion of the verification process and resolution of any deficiencies in the application information, the commissioner shall conduct an initial inspection of the premises to be licensed. After the inspection and resolution of any deficiencies found and any reinspections as may be necessary, the commissioner shall make a determination, based on all the information available, to grant or deny licensure. If the commissioner's determination is to grant the license, the applicant shall be notified and the license shall issue and remain valid for a period prescribed on the license, but not to exceed one calendar year from the date of issuance of the license. If the commissioner's determination is to deny the license, the commissioner must notify the applicant in writing of the denial and provide the specific reason for denial.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.54.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Nontransferability of license.</span></h2>
          <p>A license to operate an alkaline hydrolysis facility is not assignable or transferable and shall not be valid for any entity other than the one named. Each license issued to operate an alkaline hydrolysis facility is valid only for the location identified on the license. A 50 percent or more change in ownership or location of the alkaline hydrolysis facility automatically terminates the license. Separate licenses shall be required of two or more persons or other legal entities operating from the same location.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.54.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Display of license.</span></h2>
          <p>Each license to operate an alkaline hydrolysis facility must be conspicuously displayed in the alkaline hydrolysis facility at all times. Conspicuous display means in a location where a member of the general public within the alkaline hydrolysis facility is able to observe and read the license.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.54.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Period of licensure.</span></h2>
          <p>All licenses to operate an alkaline hydrolysis facility issued by the commissioner are valid for a period of one calendar year beginning on July 1 and ending on June 30, regardless of the date of issuance.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.54.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Reporting changes in license information.</span></h2>
          <p>Any change of license information must be reported to the commissioner, on forms provided by the commissioner, no later than 30 calendar days after the change occurs. Failure to report changes is grounds for disciplinary action.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.54.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Notification to the commissioner.</span></h2>
          <p>If the licensee is operating under a wastewater or an environmental permit or license that is subsequently revoked, denied, or terminated, the licensee shall notify the commissioner.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.54.9">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9.<span class="headnote">Application information.</span></h2>
          <p>All information submitted to the commissioner for a license to operate an alkaline hydrolysis facility is classified as licensing data under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/13.41#stat.13.41.5">13.41, subdivision 5</a>.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.149A.54.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/108/#laws.12.74.0">2013 c 108 art 12 s 74</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.149A.55">
        <h1 class="shn">149A.55 RENEWAL OF LICENSE TO OPERATE AN ALKALINE HYDROLYSIS FACILITY.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.55.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Renewal required.</span></h2>
          <p>All licenses to operate an alkaline hydrolysis facility issued by the commissioner expire on June 30 following the date of issuance of the license and must be renewed to remain valid.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.55.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Renewal procedure and documentation.</span></h2>
          <p>Licensees who wish to renew their licenses must submit to the commissioner a completed renewal application no later than June 30 following the date the license was issued. A completed renewal application includes:</p>
          <p>(1) a completed renewal application form, as provided by the commissioner; and</p>
          <p>(2) proof of liability insurance coverage or other financial documentation, as determined by the commissioner, that demonstrates the applicant's ability to respond in damages for liability arising from the ownership, maintenance, management, or operation of an alkaline hydrolysis facility.</p>
          <p class="bl">Upon receipt of the completed renewal application, the commissioner shall review and verify the information. Upon completion of the verification process and resolution of any deficiencies in the renewal application information, the commissioner shall make a determination, based on all the information available, to reissue or refuse to reissue the license. If the commissioner's determination is to reissue the license, the applicant shall be notified and the license shall issue and remain valid for a period prescribed on the license, but not to exceed one calendar year from the date of issuance of the license. If the commissioner's determination is to refuse to reissue the license, section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/149A.09">149A.09</a>, subdivision 2, applies.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.55.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Penalty for late filing.</span></h2>
          <p>Renewal applications received after the expiration date of a license will result in the assessment of a late filing penalty. The late filing penalty must be paid before the reissuance of the license and received by the commissioner no later than 31 calendar days after the expiration date of the license.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.55.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Lapse of license.</span></h2>
          <p>Licenses to operate alkaline hydrolysis facilities shall automatically lapse when a completed renewal application is not received by the commissioner within 31 calendar days after the expiration date of a license, or a late filing penalty assessed under subdivision 3 is not received by the commissioner within 31 calendar days after the expiration of a license.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.55.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Effect of lapse of license.</span></h2>
          <p>Upon the lapse of a license, the person to whom the license was issued is no longer licensed to operate an alkaline hydrolysis facility in Minnesota. The commissioner shall issue a cease and desist order to prevent the lapsed license holder from operating an alkaline hydrolysis facility in Minnesota and may pursue any additional lawful remedies as justified by the case.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.55.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Restoration of lapsed license.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner may restore a lapsed license upon receipt and review of a completed renewal application, receipt of the late filing penalty, and reinspection of the premises, provided that the receipt is made within one calendar year from the expiration date of the lapsed license and the cease and desist order issued by the commissioner has not been violated. If a lapsed license is not restored within one calendar year from the expiration date of the lapsed license, the holder of the lapsed license cannot be relicensed until the requirements in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/149A.54">149A.54</a> are met.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.55.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Reporting changes in license information.</span></h2>
          <p>Any change of license information must be reported to the commissioner, on forms provided by the commissioner, no later than 30 calendar days after the change occurs. Failure to report changes is grounds for disciplinary action.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.55.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Application information.</span></h2>
          <p>All information submitted to the commissioner by an applicant for renewal of licensure to operate an alkaline hydrolysis facility is classified as licensing data under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/13.41#stat.13.41.5">13.41, subdivision 5</a>.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.149A.55.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/108/#laws.12.75.0">2013 c 108 art 12 s 75</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.149A.56">
        <h1 class="shn">149A.56 LICENSE TO OPERATE A NATURAL ORGANIC REDUCTION FACILITY.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.56.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">License requirement.</span></h2>
          <p>This section is effective July 1, 2025. Except as provided in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/149A.01#stat.149A.01.3">149A.01, subdivision 3</a>, no person shall maintain, manage, or operate a place or premises devoted to or used in the holding and natural organic reduction of a dead human body without possessing a valid license to operate a natural organic reduction facility issued by the commissioner of health.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.56.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Requirements for natural organic reduction facility.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A natural organic reduction facility licensed under this section must consist of:</p>
          <p>(1) a building or structure that complies with applicable local and state building codes, zoning laws and ordinances, and environmental standards, and that contains one or more natural organic reduction vessels for the natural organic reduction of dead human bodies;</p>
          <p>(2) a motorized mechanical device for processing the remains in natural reduction; and</p>
          <p>(3) an appropriate refrigerated holding facility for dead human bodies awaiting natural organic reduction.</p>
          <p>(b) A natural organic reduction facility licensed under this section may also contain a display room for funeral goods.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.56.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Application procedure; documentation; initial inspection.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) An applicant for a license to operate a natural organic reduction facility shall submit a completed application to the commissioner. A completed application includes:</p>
          <p>(1) a completed application form, as provided by the commissioner;</p>
          <p>(2) proof of business form and ownership; and</p>
          <p>(3) proof of liability insurance coverage or other financial documentation, as determined by the commissioner, that demonstrates the applicant's ability to respond in damages for liability arising from the ownership, maintenance, management, or operation of a natural organic reduction facility.</p>
          <p>(b) Upon receipt of the application and appropriate fee, the commissioner shall review and verify all information. Upon completion of the verification process and resolution of any deficiencies in the application information, the commissioner shall conduct an initial inspection of the premises to be licensed. After the inspection and resolution of any deficiencies found and any reinspections as may be necessary, the commissioner shall make a determination, based on all the information available, to grant or deny licensure. If the commissioner's determination is to grant the license, the applicant shall be notified and the license shall issue and remain valid for a period prescribed on the license, but not to exceed one calendar year from the date of issuance of the license. If the commissioner's determination is to deny the license, the commissioner must notify the applicant, in writing, of the denial and provide the specific reason for denial.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.56.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Nontransferability of license.</span></h2>
          <p>A license to operate a natural organic reduction facility is not assignable or transferable and shall not be valid for any entity other than the one named. Each license issued to operate a natural organic reduction facility is valid only for the location identified on the license. A 50 percent or more change in ownership or location of the natural organic reduction facility automatically terminates the license. Separate licenses shall be required of two or more persons or other legal entities operating from the same location.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.56.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Display of license.</span></h2>
          <p>Each license to operate a natural organic reduction facility must be conspicuously displayed in the natural organic reduction facility at all times. "Conspicuous display" means in a location where a member of the general public within the natural organic reduction facility is able to observe and read the license.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.56.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Period of licensure.</span></h2>
          <p>All licenses to operate a natural organic reduction facility issued by the commissioner are valid for a period of one calendar year beginning on July 1 and ending on June 30, regardless of the date of issuance.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.56.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Reporting changes in license information.</span></h2>
          <p>Any change of license information must be reported to the commissioner, on forms provided by the commissioner, no later than 30 calendar days after the change occurs. Failure to report changes is grounds for disciplinary action.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.56.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Licensing information.</span></h2>
          <p>Section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/13.41">13.41</a> applies to data collected and maintained by the commissioner pursuant to this section.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.149A.56.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/127/#laws.58.33.0">2024 c 127 art 58 s 33</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.149A.57">
        <h1 class="shn">149A.57 RENEWAL OF LICENSE TO OPERATE A NATURAL ORGANIC REDUCTION FACILITY.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.57.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Renewal required.</span></h2>
          <p>This section is effective July 1, 2025. All licenses to operate a natural organic reduction facility issued by the commissioner expire on June 30 following the date of issuance of the license and must be renewed to remain valid.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.57.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Renewal procedure and documentation.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Licensees who wish to renew their licenses must submit to the commissioner a completed renewal application no later than June 30 following the date the license was issued. A completed renewal application includes:</p>
          <p>(1) a completed renewal application form, as provided by the commissioner; and</p>
          <p>(2) proof of liability insurance coverage or other financial documentation, as determined by the commissioner, that demonstrates the applicant's ability to respond in damages for liability arising from the ownership, maintenance, management, or operation of a natural organic reduction facility.</p>
          <p>(b) Upon receipt of the completed renewal application, the commissioner shall review and verify the information. Upon completion of the verification process and resolution of any deficiencies in the renewal application information, the commissioner shall make a determination, based on all the information available, to reissue or refuse to reissue the license. If the commissioner's determination is to reissue the license, the applicant shall be notified and the license shall issue and remain valid for a period prescribed on the license, but not to exceed one calendar year from the date of issuance of the license. If the commissioner's determination is to refuse to reissue the license, section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/149A.09">149A.09, subdivision </a>2, applies.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.57.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Penalty for late filing.</span></h2>
          <p>Renewal applications received after the expiration date of a license will result in the assessment of a late filing penalty. The late filing penalty must be paid before the reissuance of the license and received by the commissioner no later than 31 calendar days after the expiration date of the license.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.57.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Lapse of license.</span></h2>
          <p>A license to operate a natural organic reduction facility shall automatically lapse when a completed renewal application is not received by the commissioner within 31 calendar days after the expiration date of a license, or a late filing penalty assessed under subdivision 3 is not received by the commissioner within 31 calendar days after the expiration of a license.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.57.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Effect of lapse of license.</span></h2>
          <p>Upon the lapse of a license, the person to whom the license was issued is no longer licensed to operate a natural organic reduction facility in Minnesota. The commissioner shall issue a cease and desist order to prevent the lapsed license holder from operating a natural organic reduction facility in Minnesota and may pursue any additional lawful remedies as justified by the case.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.57.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Restoration of lapsed license.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner may restore a lapsed license upon receipt and review of a completed renewal application, receipt of the late filing penalty, and reinspection of the premises, provided that the receipt is made within one calendar year from the expiration date of the lapsed license and the cease and desist order issued by the commissioner has not been violated. If a lapsed license is not restored within one calendar year from the expiration date of the lapsed license, the holder of the lapsed license cannot be relicensed until the requirements in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/149A.56">149A.56</a> are met.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.57.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Reporting changes in license information.</span></h2>
          <p>Any change of license information must be reported to the commissioner, on forms provided by the commissioner, no later than 30 calendar days after the change occurs. Failure to report changes is grounds for disciplinary action.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.149A.57.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Licensing information.</span></h2>
          <p>Section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/13.41">13.41</a> applies to data collected and maintained by the commissioner pursuant to this section.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
